Appendix C
Two counterexamples for no (R Subst)

C.1.
Counterexample for (Rp Rec)

Example
5.1
.

Let
q
be a quantum variable with
\it type(q)=\mathbf{Int}
.
We define the
(+i)
-operator
U_{+i}
over the computational basis of
\mathcal{H}_{q}
by

U_{+i}\colon|x\rangle\rightarrow|x+i\rangle

and similarly for the
(-i)
-operator
U_{-i}
.
Declare the procedure
\mathit{t}oy
by

\mathbf{Proc}\ \langle\mathit{t}oy\rangle\colon\mathbf{if}\ \Box m\cdot M[q]=m\rightarrow S_{m}\ \mathbf{fi}

with
M
defined by

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\bigg\{M_{0}\triangleq\sum_{i\leq 0}|i\rangle\langle i|,\ M_{1}\triangleq\sum_{i\geq 1}|i\rangle\langle i|\bigg\}

and
\{S_{m}\}_{m=0,1}
defined by

S_{0}\triangleq\mathbf{skip},\quad S_{1}\triangleq q\;{\ast}{=}\;U_{-1};\ \mathbf{call}\ \mathit{t}oy;\ q\;{\ast}{=}\;U_{+1}

Fix
n\geq 0
. We can derive the partial correctness formula

(22)

\big\{|n\rangle_{q}\langle n|\big\}\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{t}oy\,\big\{|n\rangle_{q}\langle n|\big\}

by using (Rp Rec).
However, this is not the case if the use of (R Subst) is disallowed.

Proof.

The proof of this lemma is divided into the following two parts:

Unprovability without (R Subst).

Suppose for a contradiction that one can derive Hoare’s triple (
22
) without using (R Subst).
By (R Order), we have to show that there exist
\mathrm{PQPT}
s
P
and
Q
s.t.

\displaystyle|n\rangle_{q}\langle n|\sqsubseteq P

(23)

\displaystyle\{P\}\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{t}oy\,\{Q\}

(24)

\displaystyle Q\sqsubseteq|n\rangle_{q}\langle n|

where Hoare’s triple (
23
) is derived by using (Rp Rec).
Then we have to show that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}},\{P\}\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{t}oy\,\{Q\}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qBS}^{-}}}

\displaystyle\{P\}\,\mathbf{if}\ \Box m\cdot M[q]=m\rightarrow S_{m}\ \mathbf{fi}\,\{Q\}

where the proof system
\mathit{qBS}^{-}
is defined by

\displaystyle\mathit{qBS}^{-}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\mathit{qBS}-\mbox{(R Subst)}

By (R Case), we have to prove that there exist
\mathrm{PQPT}
s
\{P_{m}\}_{m=0,1}
s.t.

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq

\displaystyle\sum_{m}M_{m}^{{\dagger}}P_{m}M_{m}

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}},\{P\}\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{t}oy\,\{Q\}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qBS}^{-}}}

\displaystyle\{P_{0}\}\,S_{0}\,\{Q\}

(25)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}},\{P\}\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{t}oy\,\{Q\}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qBS}^{-}}}

\displaystyle\{P_{1}\}\,S_{1}\,\{Q\}

To prove Ass. (
25
), we have to show that

\{P_{1}\}\ q\;{\ast}{=}\;U_{-1}\ \{P\}\ \mathbf{call}\ \mathit{t}oy\ \{Q\}\ q\;{\ast}{=}\;U_{+1}\ \{Q\}

By (A Unit, R Order), we have to show that

\displaystyle P_{1}

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq

\displaystyle(U_{-1})^{{\dagger}}P(U_{-1})

(26)

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq

\displaystyle(U_{+1})^{{\dagger}}Q(U_{+1})

By Ass. (
24
), it follows that

(27)

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta|n\rangle_{q}\langle n|,\quad\mbox{ with }\delta\in[0,1]

This together with Ass. (
26
) implies that

\displaystyle\delta|n\rangle_{q}\langle n|

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq

\displaystyle\delta|n-1\rangle_{q}\langle n-1|

A contradiction.

Provability with (R Subst).

We introduce quantum predicate variable
\mathcal{X}_{q}
for
q
(abbr.
\mathcal{X}
)
with the
i
th main-diagonal element
\langle i|{\mathcal{X}}|i\rangle
abbreviated
\mathcal{X}_{i}
.
To prove Hoare’s triple (
22
),
by (R Subst), together with the substitution
[(|n\rangle_{q}\langle n|)/\mathcal{X}]
, it suffices to derive

\bigg\{\sum_{i\geq 0}\mathcal{X}_{i}|i\rangle\langle i|\bigg\}\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{t}oy\,\bigg\{\sum_{i\geq 0}\mathcal{X}_{i}|i\rangle\langle i|\bigg\}

By (Rp Rec), it suffices to show

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}},\begin{array}[]{c}\big\{\sum_{i\geq 0}\mathcal{X}_{i}|i\rangle\langle i|\big\}\\
\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{t}oy\\
\big\{\sum_{i\geq 0}\mathcal{X}_{i}|i\rangle\langle i|\big\}\end{array}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qBS}}}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}\{\sum_{i\geq 0}\mathcal{X}_{i}|i\rangle\langle i|\}\\
\mathbf{if}\ \Box m\cdot M[q]=m\rightarrow S_{m}\ \mathbf{fi}\\
\{\sum_{i\geq 0}\mathcal{X}_{i}|i\rangle\langle i|\}\end{array}

The routine verification of the above assertion is left to the reader,
where (R Subst) will be applied with the substitution
[(\sum_{i\geq 0}\mathcal{X}_{i+1}|i\rangle\langle i|)/\mathcal{X}]

to verifying the inner
\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{t}oy
.
∎

Remark C.1.

Closer scrutiny of the above proof reveals that
the postcondition
Q
for the outer
\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{t}oy
should have the form
\delta|n\rangle_{q}\langle n|

(
cf. Ass. (
23
,
24
,
27
)
)
;
in contrast, the postcondition
Q
for the inner
\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{t}oy
should have the form
\delta|n-1\rangle_{q}\langle n-1|

(
cf. Ass. (
24
,
26
)
)
.
Unfortunately, the variation of
Q
is beyond the expressibility of
\mathrm{QPRD}
.
By choosing
P,Q
as
\mathrm{PQPT}
s (containing parameters) and then applying (R Subst),
one can achieve the transformation of
Q
from
\delta|n\rangle_{q}\langle n|
to
\delta|n-1\rangle_{q}\langle n-1|

as pointed out above.

C.2.
Counterexample for (Rt Rec)

Example
5.2
.

Let the recursive procedure
\mathit{t}oy
be as defined in Exm.
5.1
.
Fix
n\geq 0
. We can derive the total correctness formula

(29)

\big\langle|n\rangle_{q}\langle n|\big\rangle\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{t}oy\,\big\langle|n\rangle_{q}\langle n|\big\rangle

by using (Rt Rec).
However, this is not the case if the use of (R Subst) is disallowed.

Proof.

The proof of this lemma is divided into the following two parts:

Unprovability without (R Subst).

Suppose for a contradiction that one can derive Hoare’s triple (
29
) without using (R Subst).
By (R Order), we have to show that there exist
\mathrm{PQPT}
s
P
and
Q
s.t.

\displaystyle|n\rangle_{q}\langle n|\quad\sqsubseteq\quad P

(30)

\displaystyle\langle P\rangle\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{t}oy\,\langle Q\rangle

(31)

\displaystyle Q\quad\sqsubseteq\quad|n\rangle_{q}\langle n|

where Hoare’s triple (
30
) is derived by using (Rt Rec).
Then we have to show that there exists a sequence of
\mathrm{PQPT}
s
\{P_{i}\}_{i\geq 0}^{\sqsubseteq}

with
P_{0}=0
and
P\sqsubseteq\bigsqcup_{i=0}^{\infty}P_{i}
s.t.

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}},\langle P_{i}\rangle\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{t}oy\,\langle Q\rangle

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qBS}^{-}}}

\displaystyle\langle P_{i+1}\rangle\,\mathbf{if}\ \Box m\cdot M[q]=m\rightarrow S_{m}\ \mathbf{fi}\,\langle Q\rangle

for all
i\geq 0

(
\mathit{qBS}^{-}\triangleq\mathit{qBS}-\mbox{(R Subst)}
)
.
In particular (when
i=0
), we need to show that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}},\langle 0\rangle\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{t}oy\,\langle Q\rangle

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qBS}^{-}}}

\displaystyle\langle P_{1}\rangle\,\mathbf{if}\ \Box m\cdot M[q]=m\rightarrow S_{m}\ \mathbf{fi}\,\langle Q\rangle

By (R Case), we have to show that there exist
\mathrm{PQPT}
s
\{R_{m}\}_{m=0,1}
s.t.

\displaystyle P_{1}

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0,1}M_{m}^{{\dagger}}R_{m}M_{m}

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}},\langle 0\rangle\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{t}oy\,\langle Q\rangle

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qBS}^{-}}}

\displaystyle\langle R_{0}\rangle\,S_{0}\,\langle Q\rangle

(32)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}},\langle 0\rangle\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{t}oy\,\langle Q\rangle

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qBS}^{-}}}

\displaystyle\langle R_{1}\rangle\,S_{1}\,\langle Q\rangle

To prove Ass. (
32
), we have to show that

\{R_{1}\}\ q\;{\ast}{=}\;U_{-1}\ \{0\}\ \mathbf{call}\ \mathit{t}oy_{1}\ \{Q\}\ q\;{\ast}{=}\;U_{+1}\ \{Q\}

By (A Unit, R Order), we have to show that

\displaystyle R_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

(33)

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq

\displaystyle(U_{+1})^{{\dagger}}Q(U_{+1})

By Ass. (
31
), it follows that

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta|n\rangle_{q}\langle n|,\quad\mbox{with }\delta\in[0,1]

This together with Ass. (
33
) implies that

\displaystyle\delta|n\rangle_{q}\langle n|

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq

\displaystyle\delta|n-1\rangle_{q}\langle n-1|

A contradiction.

Provability with (R Subst).

We introduce quantum predicate variable
\mathcal{X}_{q}
for
q
(abbr.
\mathcal{X}
)
with the
i
th main-diagonal element
\langle i|{\mathcal{X}}|i\rangle
(abbr.
\mathcal{X}_{i}
).
To prove Hoare’s triple (
29
),
by (R Subst), together with the substitution
[(|n\rangle_{q}\langle n|)/\mathcal{X}]
, it suffices to derive

\bigg\langle\sum_{j\geq 0}\mathcal{X}_{j}|j\rangle\langle j|\bigg\rangle\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{t}oy\,\bigg\langle\sum_{j\geq 0}\mathcal{X}_{j}|j\rangle\langle j|\bigg\rangle

By (Rt Rec), it suffices to show, for all
i\geq 0
, that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}},\begin{array}[]{c}\big\langle\sum_{0\leq j<i}\mathcal{X}_{j}|j\rangle\langle j|\big\rangle\\
\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{t}oy\\
\big\langle\sum_{j\geq 0}\mathcal{X}_{j}|j\rangle\langle j|\big\rangle\end{array}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qBS}}}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}\big\langle\sum_{0\leq j<i+1}\mathcal{X}_{j}|j\rangle\langle j|\big\rangle\\
\mathbf{if}\ \Box m\cdot M[q]=m\rightarrow S_{m}\ \mathbf{fi}\\
\big\langle\sum_{j\geq 0}\mathcal{X}_{j}|j\rangle\langle j|\big\rangle\end{array}

The routine verification of the above assertion is left to the reader,
where (R Subst) will be applied with the substitution
[(\sum_{i>j\geq 0}\mathcal{X}_{j+1}|j\rangle\langle j|)/\mathcal{X}]

to verifying the inner
\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{t}oy
.
∎

Remark C.2.

The reader might well wonder whether the contradiction of Ass. (
32
)
is due to the fixed choice of postcondition (i.e.
Q
) of
\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{t}oy
when using (Rt Rec).
In other words, one may derive Ass. (
30
)
by using a variant of (Rt Rec) with increasing postconditions, e.g.

\dfrac{\begin{array}[]{c}\exists\ \{P_{n}\}_{n\geq 0}^{\sqsubseteq},\{Q_{n}\}_{n\geq 0}^{\sqsubseteq}\mbox{ with }P_{0}=0\mbox{ s.t.}\\
\langle P_{n}\rangle\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}\,\langle Q_{n}\rangle\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qBE}}}\langle P_{n+1}\rangle\,S\,\langle Q_{n+1}\rangle\mbox{ for all }n\geq 0,\\
P\sqsubseteq\bigsqcup_{n=0}^{\infty}P_{n}\mbox{ and }\bigsqcup_{n=0}^{\infty}Q_{n}\sqsubseteq Q\end{array}}{\langle P\rangle\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}\,\langle Q\rangle}

However, we can refute it by redefining
S_{1}
as

\displaystyle S_{1}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle q\;{\ast}{=}\;U_{-1};\ \mathbf{call}\ \mathit{t}oy;\ q\;{\ast}{=}\;U_{-1};\ \mathbf{call}\ \mathit{t}oy;\ q\;{\ast}{=}\;U_{+2}

To summarize, any variant of (Rt Rec) always has a counterexample for no (R Subst).

Appendix D
Soundness and completeness

(A Bot)

\{I\}\,\mathbf{bot}\,\{P\}

(
resp.
\langle 0\rangle\,\mathbf{bot}\,\langle P\rangle
)

(A Skip)

\{P\}\,\mathbf{skip}\,\{P\}

(A Unit)

\dfrac{UU^{{\dagger}}=U^{{\dagger}}U=I_{\bar{q}}}{\{U^{\dagger}PU\}\,\bar{q}\;{\ast}{=}\;U\,\{P\}}

(A Init)

\dfrac{\sum_{i}|i\rangle_{q}\langle i|=I_{q}}{\{\sum_{i}|i\rangle_{q}\langle 0|P|0\rangle_{q}\langle i|\}\,q:=|0\rangle\,\{P\}}

(R Comp)

\dfrac{\{P\}\,S_{1}\,\{Q\}\hskip 8.50012pt\{Q\}\,S_{2}\,\{R\}}{\{P\}\,S_{1};S_{2}\,\{R\}}

(R Case)

\dfrac{\{P_{m}\}\,S_{m}\,\{Q\}\mbox{ \small for each }m}{\{\sum_{m}M_{m}^{\dagger}P_{m}M_{m}\}\,\mathbf{if}\,\{Q\}}

(R Order)

\dfrac{P\sqsubseteq P^{\prime}\hskip 8.50012pt\{P^{\prime}\}\,S\,\{Q^{\prime}\}\hskip 8.50012ptQ^{\prime}\sqsubseteq Q}{\{P\}\,S\,\{Q\}}

(R Subst)

\dfrac{\{P\}\,S\,\{Q\}}{\{P[R/\mathcal{X}]\}\,S\,\{Q[R/\mathcal{X}]\}}

(R Loc)

\dfrac{\{P\otimes I_{\bar{r}}\}\,\bar{r}:=|0\rangle;S[\bar{r}/\bar{q}]\,\{Q\otimes I_{\bar{r}}\}}{\{P\}\,\mathbf{Loc}\ \bar{q};S;\mathbf{Rel}\ \bar{q}\,\{Q\}}

(R Adap)

\dfrac{\{P\}\,S\,\{Q\}}{\{P[\bar{p}/\bar{q}]\}\,S[\bar{p}/\bar{q}]\,\{Q[\bar{p}/\bar{q}]\}}

(a)

Extended base proof system
\mathit{qBE}
.

(Rp pRec)

\dfrac{\big\{\{P_{i}\}\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}_{i}(\bar{y}_{i})\,\{Q_{i}\}\big\}_{1\leq i\leq n}\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qBE}}}\bigwedge_{1\leq i\leq n}\{P_{i}\}\,S_{i}\,\{Q_{i}\}}{\bigwedge_{1\leq i\leq n}\{P_{i}\}\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}_{i}(\bar{y}_{i})\,\{Q_{i}\}}

(Rt pRec)

\dfrac{\begin{array}[]{c}\mbox{for }1\leq i\leq n,\ \exists\ \{P_{i}^{j}\}_{j\geq 0}^{\sqsubseteq}\mbox{ with }P_{i}^{0}=0\mbox{ s.t.}\\
\big\{\langle P_{i}^{j}\rangle\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}_{i}(\bar{y}_{i})\,\langle Q_{i}\rangle\big\}_{1\leq i\leq n}\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qBE}}}\bigwedge_{1\leq i\leq n}\langle P_{i}^{j+1}\rangle\,S_{i}\,\langle Q_{i}\rangle\mbox{ for all }j\geq 0,\\
P_{i}\sqsubseteq\bigsqcup_{j=0}^{\infty}P_{i}^{j}\end{array}}{\bigwedge_{1\leq i\leq n}\langle P_{i}\rangle\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}_{i}(\bar{y}_{i})\,\langle Q_{i}\rangle}

(b)

Proof rules for parameterized procedures.

Table 16
.
Proof systems
\mathit{qPP}\triangleq\mathit{qBE}+\mbox{(Rp pRec)}
and
\mathit{qTP}\triangleq\mathit{qBE}+\mbox{(Rt pRec)}
.

Notations and definitions.

Quantum programming language
\mathit{EqPL}
is defined by:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}S&\triangleq&\mathbf{bot}\mid\mathbf{skip}\mid q:=|0\rangle\mid\bar{q}\;{\ast}{=}\;U\mid S_{1};S_{2}\mid\\
&&\mathbf{if}\ \Box m\cdot M[\bar{q}]=m\rightarrow S_{m}\ \mathbf{fi}\mid\mathbf{Loc}\ \bar{q};S;\mathbf{Rel}\ \bar{q}\end{array}

(an extension to the quantum base language
\mathit{qPL}
).
Proof system
\mathit{qBE}
for both partial and total correctness of
\mathit{EqPL}

(an extension to
\mathit{qBS}
in Tab.
4
),
partial- and total-correctness proof systems
\mathit{qPP}
and
\mathit{qTP}
for
\mathit{eRqPL}

are shown in Tab.
16
(also cf. Tab.
9
).

Organization and proof sketch

We will discuss two versions of soundness and completeness
— one for the general case and the other for the compact case.
Generally speaking,
the soundness and completeness of
\mathit{qPP}
and
\mathit{qTP}
will be reduced to that of
\mathit{qBE}

by simulating recursive procedures with their syntactic approximations
(To see this, we remark that
the inference rules for both partial and total correctness of recursive procedures are designed on the basis of
\mathit{qBE}
).
In particular,
for the proof of the completeness,
we will take the notion of the most general partial (resp. total) correctness formula

\big\{\mathit{wlp}.(\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}(\bar{y})).\mathcal{X}\big\}\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}(\bar{y})\,\big\{\mathcal{X}\big\}

(
resp.
\big\langle\mathit{wp}.(\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}(\bar{y})).\mathcal{X}\big\rangle\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}(\bar{y})\,\big\langle\mathcal{X}\big\rangle
)

whose original idea comes from the theory of classical Hoare logic
(
Francez 1992
, Chap. 6)
.
Note that we call them the most general correctness formulas because any correct Hoare’s triple
for the parameterized activation
\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}(\bar{a})
can be deduced from them by using (R Adapt) and (R Subst).

D.1.
Soundness and completeness of
\mathit{qPP}

Lemma D.0

(Soundness and completeness of
\mathit{qBE}
)
.

For any
S\in\mathit{EqPL}
and any
\mathrm{PQPT}
s
P,Q
, we have that

(35)

{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}}\vdash_{\mathit{qBE}}\{P\}\,S\,\{Q\}\iff\models_{\mathbb{I}}P\sqsubseteq\mathit{wlp}.S.Q

in particular,

(36)

{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{=}}\vdash_{\mathit{qBE}}\{P\}\,S\,\{Q\}\iff\models_{\mathbb{I}}P=\mathit{wlp}.S.Q

Proof.

Note that the unique difference between
Ass. (
35
,
36
)
lies in applications of (R Order):
the former will take its original form;
while the latter can only take the form

\dfrac{P=P^{\prime}\quad\{P^{\prime}\}\,S\,\{Q^{\prime}\}\quad Q^{\prime}=Q}{\{P\}\,S\,\{Q\}}

So, in order to prove the lemma,
it suffices to show that every proof rule
\mathcal{R}
of
\mathit{qBE}
except for (R Order)
has the following property:

every Hoare’s triple
\{P\}\,S\,\{Q\}
in the antecedent of
\mathcal{R}
satisfies
\models_{\mathbb{I}}P=\mathit{wlp}.S.Q

if, and only if,

every Hoare’s triple
\{P\}\,S\,\{Q\}
in the consequent of
\mathcal{R}
satisfies
\models_{\mathbb{I}}P=\mathit{wlp}.S.Q
.

In other words, every axiom should have the form
\{\mathit{wlp}.S.P\}\,S\,\{P\}

and every inference rule with exception of (R Order) should preserves this form bidirectionally
(That is that, the “only if” direction entails compact soundness of the rule
\mathcal{R}
,
and the “if” direction entails compact completeness of
\mathcal{R}
).
Consider the cases of (R Subst), (R Loc) and (R Adapt).
(For other cases, cf. the intuition behind
\mathit{qBS}
in Sec.
5
.)

Case: (R Loc).

It suffices to show the following two assertions

(37)

\displaystyle\models_{\mathbb{I}}

\displaystyle P\otimes I_{\bar{r}}=\mathit{wlp}.\big(\bar{r}:=|0\rangle;S[\bar{r}/\bar{q}]\big).(Q\otimes I_{\bar{r}})

(38)

\displaystyle\models_{\mathbb{I}}

\displaystyle P=\mathit{wlp}.\big(\mathbf{Loc}\ \bar{q};S;\mathbf{Rel}\ \bar{q}\big).Q

are equivalent. By definition of
\mathit{wlp}
(cf. Tab.
14
), we have that

\displaystyle\mathit{wlp}.\big(\bar{r}:=|0\rangle;S[\bar{r}/\bar{q}]\big).(Q\otimes I_{\bar{r}})

\displaystyle=\mathit{wlp}.(\bar{r}:=|0\rangle).\big(\mathit{wlp}.S[\bar{r}/\bar{q}].(Q\otimes I_{\bar{r}})\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}|i\rangle_{\bar{r}}\langle 0|\big(\mathit{wlp}.S[\bar{r}/\bar{q}].(Q\otimes I_{\bar{r}})\big)|0\rangle_{\bar{r}}\langle i|

\displaystyle=\langle 0|_{\bar{r}}\big(\mathit{wlp}.S[\bar{r}/\bar{q}].(Q\otimes I_{\bar{r}})\big)|0\rangle_{\bar{r}}\otimes I_{\bar{r}}

\displaystyle=\big(\mathit{wlp}.(\mathbf{Loc}\ \bar{q};S;\mathbf{Rel}\ \bar{q}).Q\big)\otimes I_{\bar{r}}

Then equivalence of Ass. (
37
,
38
)
follows from the convention that
P\otimes I_{\bar{r}}=P
.

Case: (R Subst, R Adap).

By Thm.
4.15
.
∎

Theorem D.2

(Soundness and Completeness of
\mathit{qPP}
)
.

\mathit{qPP}
is both sound and complete.
That is, for any quantum program
S\in\mathit{eRqPL}
and any
\mathrm{PQPT}
s
P,Q
, we have that

{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}}\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qPP}}}\{P\}\,S\,\{Q\}\iff\models_{\mathbb{I}}\{P\}\,S\,\{Q\}

Proof.

The proof is divided into two parts: one for
\implies
and the other for
\impliedby
.

(
\implies
).

The soundness of
\mathit{qPP}
can be reduced to that of
\mathit{qBE}

by simulating recursive procedures with their syntactic approximations.
To see this, suppose that

\{P_{k}\}\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}_{k}(\bar{y}_{k})\,\{Q_{k}\}

is deduced by using (Rp pRec).
Then we have to prove that

(39)

\displaystyle\models_{\mathbb{I}}

\displaystyle\{P_{k}\}\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}_{k}(\bar{y}_{k})\,\{Q_{k}\}

By the supposition,
there are parameterized procedures
\mathit{proc}_{i}(\bar{y}_{i})
with bodies
S_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq n
and
i\neq k

(Here it is required that
1\leq k\leq n
)
and a set of
\mathrm{PQPT}
s
\{P_{i},Q_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq n}^{i\neq k}
s.t.

(40)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}},\Big\{\{P_{i}\}\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}_{i}(\bar{y}_{i})\,\{Q_{i}\}\Big\}_{1\leq i\leq n}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qBE}}}

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{1\leq i\leq n}\{P_{i}\}\,S_{i}\,\{Q_{i}\}

Claim 1
.

For every
j\geq 0
, it is the case that

(41)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qBE}}}

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{1\leq i\leq n}\{P_{i}\}\,S_{i}^{(j)}\,\{Q_{i}\}

Proof of Claim
1
.

By induction on
j
.

(Basis).
By (A Bot) and (R Order), together with the fact that
(P_{i}\sqsubseteq I)\in{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}}
.

(Induction).

Recalling the definition of
S_{i}^{(j+1)}
, i.e.

\displaystyle S_{i}^{(j+1)}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle S_{i}\big[\ldots,\big(\mathbf{skip};S_{l}^{(j)}[\bar{a}_{l_{m}}/\bar{y}_{l}]\big)\big/\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}_{l}(\bar{a}_{l_{m}}),\ldots\big]

the inductive step can be done by simulating the proof of Ass. (
40
)
with
\mathbf{skip};S_{l}^{(j)}

(
resp.
\mathbf{skip};S_{l}^{(j)}[\bar{a}_{l_{m}}/\bar{y}_{l}]
)
in place of
\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}_{l}(\bar{y}_{l})

(
resp.
\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}_{l}(\bar{a}_{l_{m}})
)
.

By Def.
4.17
, together with definition of
\mathit{wlp}
,
the proof of Ass. (
39
) is reduced to proving

\displaystyle\models_{\mathbb{I}}

\displaystyle\{P_{k}\}\,S_{k}^{(j)}\,\{Q_{k}\}

for all
j\geq 0
,
following from soundness of
\mathit{qBE}
(cf. Lem.
D.1
) and Claim
1
.

(
\impliedby
).

By (R Order), together with Def.
4.17
, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{=}}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qPP}}}

\displaystyle\{\mathit{wlp}.S.Q\}\,S\,\{Q\}

for any
S\in\mathit{eRqPL}
and any
\mathrm{PQPT}
s
Q
.
In the following, we only consider the case of parameterized activation,
i.e.
S\equiv\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}_{k}(\bar{a}_{k})
(Cf. Lem.
D.1
for other cases).

By (R Adap), together with (R Subst), it suffices to show that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{=}}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qPP}}}

\displaystyle\big\{\mathit{wlp}.\big(\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}_{k}(\bar{y}_{k})\big).\mathcal{X}_{k}\big\}\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}_{k}(\bar{y}_{k})\,\big\{\mathcal{X}_{k}\big\}

where
\mathcal{X}_{k}
is a quantum predicate variable covering program variables involved.
By (Rp pRec), it suffices to show that

(42)

{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{=}},\bigg\{\begin{array}[]{c}\big\{\mathit{wlp}.\big(\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}_{i}(\bar{y}_{i})\big).\mathcal{X}_{i}\big\}\\
\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}_{i}(\bar{y}_{i})\\
\big\{\mathcal{X}_{i}\big\}\end{array}\bigg\}_{1\leq i\leq n}\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qBE}}}\bigwedge_{1\leq i\leq n}\begin{array}[]{c}\big\{\mathit{wlp}.\big(\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}_{i}(\bar{y}_{i})\big).\mathcal{X}_{i}\big\}\\
S_{i}\\
\big\{\mathcal{X}_{i}\big\}\end{array}

where
\mathit{proc}_{i}(\bar{y}_{i})
with
1\leq i\leq n
and
i\neq k
are parameterized procedures with bodies
S_{i}
,
(Here it is required that
1\leq k\leq n
)
and
\{\mathcal{X}_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq n}^{i\neq k}
is a set of quantum predicate variables.
Observe that every Hoare’s triple
\{P^{\prime}\}\,S^{\prime}\,\{Q^{\prime}\}
in the proof of Ass. (
42
)
should satisfy the condition that
\models_{\mathbb{I}}P^{\prime}=\mathit{wlp}.S^{\prime}.Q^{\prime}
:
for those
S^{\prime}\equiv\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}_{i}(\bar{p}_{i})
,
Hoare’s triple
\{P^{\prime}\}\,S^{\prime}\,\{Q^{\prime}\}
can be deduced from

\{\mathit{wlp}.\big(\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}_{i}(\bar{y}_{i})\big).\mathcal{X}_{i}\}\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{proc}_{i}(\bar{y}_{i})\,\{\mathcal{X}_{i}\}

by using (R Adapt) and (R Subst); for other cases of
S^{\prime}
, resort to Lem.
D.1
.
Note that the case analysis entails that such a proof indeed exists.
This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Theorem D.3

(Compact Soundness and Completeness of
\mathit{qPP}
)
.

\mathit{qPP}
is compactly complete but not compactly sound.
That is, for any quantum program
S\in\mathit{eRqPL}
and any
\mathrm{PQPT}
s
P,Q
, we have that

(43)

{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{=}}\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qPP}}}\{P\}\,S\,\{Q\}\impliedby\models_{\mathbb{I}}P=\mathit{wlp}.S.Q

However, there are quantum program
S\in\mathit{eRqPL}
and
\mathrm{PQPT}
s
P,Q
s.t.

(44)

{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{=}}\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qPP}}}\{P\}\,S\,\{Q\}\mbox{ but }\models_{\mathbb{I}}P\sqsubset\mathit{wlp}.S.Q

Proof.

Note that the proof of Ass. (
43
)
can be adapted from that of Thm.
D.2
(
\impliedby
).
To prove Ass. (
44
),
let
\mathbf{call}\ P_{\mathbf{bot}}
be as defined in Exam.
3.1
,
and
P,Q

\mathrm{PQPT}
s with
\models_{\mathbb{I}}P\sqsubset I
.
It’s trivial that

\displaystyle\{P\}\,\mathbf{call}\ P_{\mathbf{bot}}\,\{Q\}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qBE}}}

\displaystyle\{P\}\,\mathbf{call}\ P_{\mathbf{bot}}\,\{Q\}

By (Rp pRec), it follows that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{=}}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qPP}}}

\displaystyle\{P\}\,\mathbf{call}\ P_{\mathbf{bot}}\,\{Q\}

However, by definition of
\mathit{wlp}
(cf. Tab.
14
), we have that

\displaystyle\models_{\mathbb{I}}

\displaystyle P\sqsubset I=\mathit{wlp}.(\mathbf{call}\ P_{\mathbf{bot}}).Q

This completes the proof.
∎

D.2.
Soundness and completeness of
\mathit{qTP}

Lemma D.0

(Soundness and completeness of
\mathit{qBE}
)
.

For any
S\in\mathit{EqPL}
and any
\mathrm{PQPT}
s
P,Q
, we have that

(45)

{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}}\vdash_{\mathit{qBE}}\langle P\rangle\,S\,\langle Q\rangle\iff\models_{\mathbb{I}}P\sqsubseteq\mathit{wp}.S.Q

in particular,

(46)

{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{=}}\vdash_{\mathit{qBE}}\langle P\rangle\,S\,\langle Q\rangle\iff\models_{\mathbb{I}}P=\mathit{wp}.S.Q

Proof.

Adapted from the proof of Lem.
D.1
with
\mathit{wp}
in place of
\mathit{wlp}
.
∎

Theorem D.5

(Soundness and completeness of
\mathit{qTP}
)
.

For any quantum program
S\in\mathit{eRqPL}
and any
\mathrm{PQPT}
s
P,Q
, we have that

(47)

{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}}\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qTP}}}\langle P\rangle\,S\,\langle Q\rangle\iff\models_{\mathbb{I}}P\sqsubseteq\mathit{wp}.S.Q

in particular,

(48)

{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{=}}\vdash_{\mathit{qTP}}\langle P\rangle\,S\,\langle Q\rangle\iff\models_{\mathbb{I}}P=\mathit{wp}.S.Q

Proof.

Similar to the proof of Thm.
D.2
.
∎

Theorem D.6

(Reasoning about
\mathit{eRqPL}
with probabilities)
.

For any quantum program
S\in\mathit{eRqPL}
, any
\mathrm{PQPT}
s
P,Q
and any
\delta\in[0,1]
, it is the case that

(49)

{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}}\vdash_{\mathit{qTP}}\langle\delta P\rangle\,S\,\langle Q\rangle\mbox{ if and only if }\forall\rho.\ \mathit{tr}(P\rho)=1\implies\mathit{tr}\big(Q\llbracket S\rrbracket(\rho)\big)\geq\delta

in particular,

(50)

{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{=}}\vdash_{\mathit{qTP}}\langle\delta P\rangle\,S\,\langle Q\rangle\mbox{ if and only if }\forall\rho.\ \mathit{tr}(P\rho)=1\implies\mathit{tr}\big(Q\llbracket S\rrbracket(\rho)\big)=\delta

Proof.

In the following, we only provide the proof for Ass. (
49
).

(
The proof of Ass. (
50
)
can be adapted from the proof of Ass. (
49
) with
\geq
in place of
=
.
)

By Thm.
D.5
, it follows that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{=}}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{qTP}}

\displaystyle\langle\delta P\rangle\,S\,\langle Q\rangle

if, and only if,

\displaystyle\models_{\mathbb{I}}

\displaystyle\delta P=\mathit{wp}.S.Q

By Thm.
B.2
, the last assertion is equivalent to saying that

\displaystyle\models_{\mathbb{I}}

\displaystyle\delta P=\llbracket S\rrbracket^{*}(Q)

By Lem.
2.3
, the last assertion is equivalent to saying that

\forall\rho.\ \mathit{tr}(\delta P\rho)=\mathit{tr}\big(\llbracket S\rrbracket^{*}(Q)\rho\big)

By definition of Schrödinger-Heisenberg dual, the last assertion is equivalent to saying that

\forall\rho.\ \mathit{tr}(\delta P\rho)=\mathit{tr}\big(Q\llbracket S\rrbracket(\rho)\big)

By an easy transformation, the last assertion is equivalent to saying that

\forall\rho.\ \mathit{tr}(P\rho)=1\implies\mathit{tr}\big(Q\llbracket S\rrbracket(\rho)\big)=\delta

This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Appendix E
Fixed-point Grover’s search

E.1.
Basic idea of the algorithm

Let
|s\rangle
and
|t\rangle
be the respective starting and target states in a Hilbert space,
where
|s\rangle
is possibly superposed,
and
|t\rangle
is a (not necessarily uniform) superposition of all the possible solutions.
The core of the algorithm is to design a search engine
— a series of unitary operators
\{V_{n}\}_{n\geq 0}
(given by an inductive definition)

(51)

\begin{array}[]{c}V_{0}\triangleq V,\quad V_{n+1}\triangleq V_{n}R_{s}V_{n}^{{\dagger}}R_{t}V_{n}\end{array}

where the
\frac{\pi}{3}
-phase shifts (i.e., unitary operators)

R_{s}
and
R_{t}
for
|s\rangle
and
|t\rangle
are defined as

(52)

\begin{array}[]{cc}R_{s}\triangleq I-\big(1-\exp(i\frac{\pi}{3})\big)|s\rangle\langle s|,&R_{t}\triangleq I-\big(1-\exp(i\frac{\pi}{3})\big)|t\rangle\langle t|\end{array}

such that the resulting state
V_{n}|s\rangle
after applying
V_{n}
to
|s\rangle

converges monotonically to
|t\rangle
as
n
approaches infinity, that is to say,

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}{V_{n}|s\rangle}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|t\rangle

As the last step, we fetch information of the solution
|t\rangle
by a measurement on
V_{n}|s\rangle
.

Note that
V
can be selected arbitrarily.
Suppose that
V
drives the state vector from
s
to
t
with a probability of
(1-\epsilon)
, i.e.

\displaystyle||\langle t|V|s\rangle||^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\epsilon)

Then it is straightforward but tedious to show that
the resulting state
V_{n}|s\rangle
after applying
V_{n}
deviates from
t
with a probability of
\epsilon^{3^{n}}
, i.e.

\displaystyle||\langle t|V_{n}|s\rangle||^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\epsilon^{3^{n}})

hence reducing the error probability from
\epsilon
to
\epsilon^{3^{n}}
.

E.2.
Quantum programs of the algorithm

\mathbf{Proc}\ \mathit{qSearch}\colon S

S

\triangleq

\mathbf{if}\ \Box m\cdot M[q_{1}]=m\rightarrow S_{m}\ \mathbf{fi}

S_{0}

\triangleq

q_{2}\;{\ast}{=}\;V

S_{1}

\triangleq

q_{1}\;{\ast}{=}\;U_{-1};

\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{qSearch};

q_{2}\;{\ast}{=}\;R_{t};

\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{qSearch\_dag};

q_{2}\;{\ast}{=}\;R_{s};

\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{qSearch};

q_{1}\;{\ast}{=}\;U_{+1}

(a)

\mathit{qSearch}

\mathbf{Proc}\ \mathit{qSearch\_dag}\colon S^{\prime}

S^{\prime}

\triangleq

\mathbf{if}\ \Box m\cdot M[q_{1}]=m\rightarrow S_{m}^{\prime}\ \mathbf{fi}

S_{0}^{\prime}

\triangleq

q_{2}\;{\ast}{=}\;V^{\dagger}

S_{1}^{\prime}

\triangleq

q_{1}\;{\ast}{=}\;U_{-1};

\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{qSearch\_dag};

q_{2}\;{\ast}{=}\;R_{s}^{{\dagger}};

\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{qSearch};

q_{2}\;{\ast}{=}\;R_{t}^{{\dagger}};

\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{qSearch\_dag};

q_{1}\;{\ast}{=}\;U_{+1}

(b)

\mathit{qSearch\_dag}

Table 17
.

\mathit{qSearch}
and
\mathit{qSearch\_dag}
implement the search engine
V_{n}
and its adjoint
V_{n}^{{\dagger}}
.

To be precise, we define the involved Hilbert spaces carefully.
Define the search space
\mathcal{H}_{s}
to be an
N
-dimensional Hilbert space
with orthonormal basis states
\big\{|n\rangle\colon 0\leq n<N\big\}
,
for encoding a database with solutions represented as
|t\rangle
.
Define the counting space
\mathcal{H}_{c}
to be a
2^{m}
-dimensional Hilbert space
with orthonormal basis states
\big\{|i\rangle\colon 0\leq i<2^{m}\big\}
,
to encode an upper-bounded set of natural numbers.
Note that we use orthonormal basis states of
\mathcal{H}_{c}

to encode the counter values of the search engine (
m
should be large enough),
and therefore use a quantum variable of
\mathcal{H}_{c}

to model the counter instead of a classical counter variable.
We then define
(+i)
-operator
U_{+i}
of
\mathcal{H}_{c}
by

\begin{array}[]{c}U_{+i}\colon|x\rangle\rightarrow|(x+i)\mod 2^{m}\rangle,\end{array}

to model the classical modular
(+i)
-operator,
and similarly for
(-i)
-operator
U_{-i}
.

Now the state space of the search algorithm is
\mathcal{H}_{c}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{s}
.
We set the initial state to be
|n\rangle|s\rangle
.
To achieve this,
we apply unitary operators
U_{+n}
and
U_{s}
to
|0\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{c}}
and
|0\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{s}}
, respectively.
Here, the unitary operator
U_{s}
is artificially devised to prepare the starting state
|s\rangle
.

In each step of the search procedure:

(1) Prepare the counting state
|n\rangle
and starting state
|s\rangle
by applying
U_{+n}\otimes U_{s}
to
|0\rangle|0\rangle
.

(2) Apply the search engine
V_{n}
, as defined above,
to the starting state
|s\rangle
with the counting state
|n\rangle
to determine the recursion depth.
To do so, we first perform the measurement

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\bigg\{M_{0}\triangleq|0\rangle\langle 0|,\;M_{1}\triangleq\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}-1}|i\rangle\langle i|\bigg\}

on the counting state
|n\rangle
; then execute the following depending on the measurement outcome.

•

if the outcome is
0
, the search procedure apply
V
;

•

otherwise, the search procedure applies
V_{n-1}R_{s}V_{n-1}^{{\dagger}}R_{t}V_{n-1}

(3) Measure the resulting state
V_{n}|s\rangle
to obtain the information of solution.
Here we can choose a standard (computational) basis measurement,
if elements of the database are encoded as a standard basis state.

Let
q_{1}
and
q_{2}
be respective quantum variables over
\mathcal{H}_{c}
and
\mathcal{H}_{s}
.
Recursive quantum procedure
\mathit{qSearch}
for the search engine is designed in Tab.
17
.

E.3.
Partial correctness

\{A_{0,0}|0\rangle\langle 0|\otimes B\}

(a)

q_{2}\;{\ast}{=}\;V

\{A_{0,0}|0\rangle\langle 0|\otimes V_{0}BV_{0}^{\dagger}\}

(A Unit)

(b)

\{\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-1}A_{i,i}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes V_{i}BV_{i}^{\dagger}\}

{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}}

\{\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}-1}A_{i,i}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes B\}

(c)

q_{1}\;{\ast}{=}\;U_{-1};

\{\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-2}A_{i,i}^{\prime}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes B\}

(A Unit)

(d)

\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{qSearch};

\{\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-2}A_{i,i}^{\prime}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes V_{i}BV_{i}^{\dagger}\}

\mathit{Prem}_{1}
, (R Subst)

(e)

q_{2}\;{\ast}{=}\;R_{t};

\{\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-2}A_{i,i}^{\prime}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes R_{t}V_{i}BV_{i}^{\dagger}R_{t}^{\dagger}\}

(A Unit)

(f)

\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{qSearch\_dag};

\{\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-2}A_{i,i}^{\prime}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes V_{i}^{\dagger}R_{t}V_{i}BV_{i}^{\dagger}R_{t}^{\dagger}V_{i}\}

\mathit{Prem}_{2}
, (R Subst)

(g)

q_{2}\;{\ast}{=}\;R_{s};

\{\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-2}A_{i,i}^{\prime}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes R_{s}V_{i}^{\dagger}R_{t}V_{i}BV_{i}^{\dagger}R_{t}^{\dagger}V_{i}R_{s}^{\dagger}\}

(A Unit)

(h)

\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{qSearch}

\{\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-2}A_{i,i}^{\prime}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes V_{i}R_{s}V_{i}^{\dagger}R_{t}V_{i}BV_{i}^{\dagger}R_{t}^{\dagger}V_{i}R_{s}^{\dagger}V_{i}^{\dagger}\}

\mathit{Prem}_{1}
, (R Subst)

(i)

q_{1}\;{\ast}{=}\;U_{+1}

\{\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}-1}A_{i,i}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes V_{i}BV_{i}^{\dagger}\}

(A Unit)

(j)

\{\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-1}A_{i,i}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes V_{i}BV_{i}^{\dagger}\}

{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}}

(k)

\{\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-1}A_{i,i}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes B\}\,S\,\{\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-1}A_{i,i}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes V_{i}BV_{i}^{\dagger}\}

(a-b, c-j, R Case)

Table 18.
Proof of Ass. (
55
).

We claim that, on input
|n\rangle_{q_{1}}\otimes|s\rangle_{q_{2}}
,
quantum activation statement
\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{qSearch}
(cf. Tab.
17
for
\mathit{qSearch}
)
executes with output
|n\rangle_{q_{1}}\otimes V_{n}|s\rangle_{q_{2}}
(if terminates).
Formally speaking, the claim can be expressed as a partially correct quantum Hoare’s triple:

\displaystyle\models_{\mathbb{I}}

\displaystyle\big\{|n\rangle_{q_{1}}\langle n|\otimes|s\rangle_{q_{2}}\langle s|\big\}\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{qSearch}\,\big\{|n\rangle_{q_{1}}\langle n|\otimes V_{n}|s\rangle_{q_{2}}\langle s|V_{n}^{\dagger}\big\}

By soundness and completeness of
\mathit{qPP}
, it is to say that

(53)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qPP}}}

\displaystyle\big\{|n\rangle_{q_{1}}\langle n|\otimes|s\rangle_{q_{2}}\langle s|\big\}\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{qSearch}\,\big\{|n\rangle_{q_{1}}\langle n|\otimes V_{n}|s\rangle_{q_{2}}\langle s|V_{n}^{\dagger}\big\}

Let
A
be a quantum predicate variable on
\mathcal{H}_{c}
,
and
B
a quantum predicate variable on
\mathcal{H}_{s}
.
The
(i,j)
-component
\langle i|A|j\rangle
of
A
is abbreviated as
A_{i,j}
,
so
A=\sum_{i,j}A_{i,j}|i\rangle\langle j|
.
Following we shall use the main-diagonal elements of
A
to encode classical information,
which is in accordance with the fact that quantum variable
q_{1}
is used classically.
Define
\mathrm{PQPT}

A^{\prime}
by

\displaystyle A^{\prime}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2^{m}-1}A_{i,i}|i-1\rangle\langle i-1|

To prove Ass. (
53
), by (Subst Rule), together with the simultaneous substitution

\big[|n\rangle_{q_{1}}\langle n|/A,\ |s\rangle_{q_{2}}\langle s|/B\big]

it suffices to show that

(54)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qPP}}}

\displaystyle\bigg\{\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-1}A_{i,i}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes B\bigg\}\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{qSearch}\,\bigg\{\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-1}A_{i,i}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes V_{i}BV_{i}^{\dagger}\bigg\}

Intuitively, the precondition (resp. postcondition) of the Hoare’s triple in Ass. (
54
) says that
the control flow arrives at each recursion depth
0\leq i\leq 2^{m}-1
(denoted by variable
q_{1}
) of procedure
\mathit{qSearch}
with probability
A_{i,i}
, and at depth
i
, the state of variable
q_{2}
should satisfy
the predicate
B
(resp.
V_{i}BV_{i}^{\dagger}
).

Define a set of premises
\big\{\mathit{Prem}_{i}\big\}_{i=1,2}
by

\displaystyle\mathit{Prem}_{1}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\bigg\{\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-1}A_{i,i}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes B\bigg\}\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{qSearch}\,\bigg\{\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-1}A_{i,i}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes V_{i}BV_{i}^{\dagger}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\mathit{Prem}_{2}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\bigg\{\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-1}A_{i,i}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes B\bigg\}\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{qSearch\_dag}\,\bigg\{\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-1}A_{i,i}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes V_{i}^{\dagger}BV_{i}\bigg\}

To prove Ass. (
54
), by (Rp pRec), it suffices to show that

(55)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}},\big\{\mathit{Prem}_{i}\big\}_{i=1,2}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qBE}}}

\displaystyle\bigg\{\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-1}A_{i,i}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes B\bigg\}\,S\,\bigg\{\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-1}A_{i,i}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes V_{i}BV_{i}^{\dagger}\bigg\}

illustrated in Tab.
18
, and that

(56)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}},\big\{\mathit{Prem}_{i}\big\}_{i=1,2}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qBE}}}

\displaystyle\bigg\{\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-1}A_{i,i}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes B\bigg\}\,S^{\prime}\,\bigg\{\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-1}A_{i,i}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes V_{i}^{\dagger}BV_{i}\bigg\}

whose proof is similar to that of Ass. (
55
) and is left as an exercise to the reader.

Note that for Hoare’s triple (d) in Tab.
18
, we use the substitution

\big[A^{\prime}\big/A\big]

for (f), we use the substitution

\big[A^{\prime}\big/A,\ \big(R_{t}V_{i}BV_{i}^{\dagger}R_{t}^{\dagger}\big)\big/B\big]

for (h), we use the substitution

\big[A^{\prime}\big/A,\ \big(R_{s}V_{i}^{\dagger}R_{t}V_{i}BV_{i}^{\dagger}R_{t}^{\dagger}V_{i}R_{s}^{\dagger}\big)\big/B\big]

E.4.
Total correctness

We claim that quantum activation statement
\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{qSearch}
(cf. Tab.
17
for
\mathit{qSearch}
),
on input
|n\rangle_{q_{1}}\otimes|s\rangle_{q_{2}}
, always terminates with output
|n\rangle_{q_{1}}\otimes V_{n}|s\rangle_{q_{2}}
.
In a formal way, the claim can be expressed as a totally correct quantum Hoare’s triple:

\displaystyle\models_{\mathbb{I}}

\displaystyle\big\langle|n\rangle_{q_{1}}\langle n|\otimes|s\rangle_{q_{2}}\langle s|\big\rangle\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{qSearch}\,\big\langle|n\rangle_{q_{1}}\langle n|\otimes V_{n}|s\rangle_{q_{2}}\langle s|V_{n}^{\dagger}\big\rangle

By soundness and completeness of
\mathit{qTP}
, it is to say that

(57)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qTP}}}

\displaystyle\big\langle|n\rangle_{q_{1}}\langle n|\otimes|s\rangle_{q_{2}}\langle s|\big\rangle\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{qSearch}\,\big\langle|n\rangle_{q_{1}}\langle n|\otimes V_{n}|s\rangle_{q_{2}}\langle s|V_{n}^{\dagger}\big\rangle

Recall from Subsec.
E.3
that
A
is a quantum predicate variable on
\mathcal{H}_{c}
,
and
B
a quantum predicate variable on
\mathcal{H}_{s}
.
Note that
(i,j)
-component
\langle i|A|j\rangle
of
A
is abbreviated as
A_{i,j}
.
To prove Ass. (
57
), by (R Subst), together with the simultaneous substitution

\big[|n\rangle_{q_{1}}\langle n|/A,\ |s\rangle_{q_{2}}\langle s|/B\big]

it suffices to show that

(58)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qTP}}}

\displaystyle\bigg\langle\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-1}A_{i,i}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes B\bigg\rangle\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{qSearch}\,\bigg\langle\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-1}A_{i,i}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes V_{i}BV_{i}^{\dagger}\bigg\rangle

Define a sequence of
\mathrm{PQPT}
s
\big\{P_{j}[A,B]\big\}_{j\geq 0}^{\sqsubseteq}
by

\displaystyle P_{j}[A,B]

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{i=0}^{j}A_{i,i}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes B&\hbox{if $0\leq j<2^{m}$}\\
\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-1}A_{i,i}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes B&\hbox{if $j\geq 2^{m}$}\end{array}\right.

Then a set of premises
\big\{\mathit{Prem}_{i}^{j}\big\}^{j\geq 0}_{i=1,2}
is defined by

\displaystyle\mathit{Prem}_{1}^{j}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\bigg\langle P_{j}[A,B]\bigg\rangle\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{qSearch}\,\bigg\langle\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-1}A_{i,i}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes V_{i}BV_{i}^{\dagger}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle\mathit{Prem}_{2}^{j}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\bigg\langle P_{j}[A,B]\bigg\rangle\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{qSearch\_dag}\,\bigg\langle\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-1}A_{i,i}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes V_{i}^{\dagger}BV_{i}\bigg\rangle

By (Rt pRec), it suffices to show, for all
j\geq 0
, that

(62)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}},\big\{Prem_{i}^{j}\big\}_{i=1,2}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qBE}}}

\displaystyle\bigg\langle P_{j+1}[A,B]\bigg\rangle\,S\,\bigg\langle\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-1}A_{i,i}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes V_{i}BV_{i}^{\dagger}\bigg\rangle

(63)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}},\big\{Prem_{i}^{j}\big\}_{i=1,2}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qBE}}}

\displaystyle\bigg\langle P_{j+1}[A,B]\bigg\rangle\,S^{\prime}\,\bigg\langle\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-1}A_{i,i}|i\rangle\langle i|\otimes V_{i}^{\dagger}BV_{i}\bigg\rangle

We remark that the proof of Ass. (
62
)
can be adapted from that of (
55
) [cf. Tab.
18
]
by replacing the superscript (
2^{m}-1
) of some necessary but not all summation operators (including those in the definition of
A^{\prime}
) with
(j+1)
.
Moreover, Ass. (
63
) can be proved similarly to (
62
).
We leave it as an exercise to the reader.

Appendix F
Recursive quantum Fourier sampling

F.1.
Problem description

Let us first briefly recall recursive quantum Fourier sampling,
following the literature
(
McKague 2012
)
.
We begin by defining a type of tree.
Let
n,l
be positive integers and consider a symmetric tree where each node,
except the leaves, has
2^{n}
children, and the depth is
l
.
Let the root be labelled by
(\emptyset)
.
The root’s children are labelled
(x_{1})
with
x_{1}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
.
Each child of
(x_{1})
is, in turn, labelled
(x_{1},x_{2})
with
x_{2}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
.
We continue until we have reached the leaves, which are labelled by
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{l})
.
Thus each node’s label can be thought of as a path describing how to find the node from the root.

Now we add the Fourier component to the tree.
We begin by fixing an efficiently computable function
g\colon\{0,1\}^{n}\rightarrow\{0,1\}
.
With each node of the tree
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})
we associate a “secret” string
s_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
.
These secrets are promised to obey

\displaystyle g(s_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})})

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle s_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k-1})}\cdot x_{k}\mod 2

for
k\geq 1
.
(
Here we take
s_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k-1})}
to mean
s_{(\emptyset)}
if
k=1
.
)

In this way, each node’s secret encodes one bit of information about its parent’s secret.
Suppose that we are given an oracle
o\colon(\{0,1\}^{n})^{l}\rightarrow\{0,1\}
which behaves as

\displaystyle o(x_{1},\ldots,x_{l})

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle g\big(s_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{l})}\big)

Note that
o
works for the leaves of the tree
only
.
Our goal is to find
g(s_{(\emptyset)})
.
This is the recursive Fourier sampling problem (
\mathcal{RFS}
).

F.2.
Quantum solution

\mathbf{Proc}\ \mathit{RQFS}(q,Y)\colon\mathbf{if}\ \Box m\cdot M[q]=m\rightarrow S_{m}\ \mathbf{fi}

S_{0}

\triangleq

q\;{\ast}{=}\;U_{+1};

\mathbf{Loc}\ \mathbb{X}[q],Y^{\prime};

\big(\mathbb{X}[q],Y^{\prime}\big)\;{\ast}{=}\;H^{\otimes n}\otimes HX;

\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{RQFS}(q,Y^{\prime});

\mathbb{X}[q]\;{\ast}{=}\;H^{\otimes n};

\big(\mathbb{X}[q],Y\big)\;{\ast}{=}\;\mathcal{G};

\mathbb{X}[q]\;{\ast}{=}\;H^{\otimes n};

\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{RQFS}(q,Y^{\prime});

\big(\mathbb{X}[q],Y^{\prime}\big)\;{\ast}{=}\;H^{\otimes n}\otimes XH;

\mathbf{Rel}\ \mathbb{X}[q],Y^{\prime};

q\;{\ast}{=}\;U_{-1}

S_{1}

\triangleq

\big(\mathbb{X}[1],\ldots,\mathbb{X}[l],Y\big)\;{\ast}{=}\;\mathcal{O}

S_{2}

\triangleq

\mathbf{bot}

(a)

Recursive procedure
\mathit{RQFS}
.

\mathit{m}ain

\triangleq

(p,Z):=|0\rangle
;

\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{RQFS}(p,Z)
;

\mathbf{if}\ \Box m\cdot M^{\prime}[Z]=m\rightarrow\mathbf{skip}\ \mathbf{fi}

(b)

Main program
\mathit{m}ain
.

Table 19
.
Programming recursive quantum Fourier sampling.

We now consider a quantum solution to
\mathcal{RFS}
.
Define the descendant space
\mathcal{H}_{d}
to be the
2^{n}
-dimensional Hilbert space with orthonormal basis states
—
\big\{|i\rangle\colon 0\leq i<2^{n}\big\}
— to index each of
2^{n}
children for any parental node.
Define the counting space
\mathcal{H}_{c}
to be the
2^{m}
-dimensional Hilbert space with orthonormal basis states
—
\big\{|i\rangle\colon 0\leq i<2^{m}\big\}
— for indexing the depth of the tree, such that
l<2^{m}
.
Define
(+i)
-operator
U_{+i}
of
\mathcal{H}_{c}
by

U_{+i}\colon|x\rangle\rightarrow|(x+i)\mod 2^{m}\rangle

and similarly for
(-i)
-operator
U_{-i}
.

Let
p,q
be quantum (individual) variables over
\mathcal{H}_{c}
,
Y,Y^{\prime},Z
quantum variables over
\mathcal{H}_{2}
,
and
\mathbb{X}
(resp.,
\mathbb{Y}
) a quantum array-like variable over
\mathcal{H}_{d}
(resp.,
\mathcal{H}_{2}
)
with one argument, say
q
, indexing each component of the array,
s.t. each component
\mathbb{X}[q]
(resp.,
\mathbb{Y}[q]
) acts like a quantum variable over
\mathcal{H}_{d}
(resp.,
\mathcal{H}_{2}
).
We shall treat
\mathbb{X}[|i\rangle]
(resp.,
\mathbb{Y}[|i\rangle]
) as
\mathbb{X}[i]
(resp.,
\mathbb{Y}[i]
) for simplicity.
The starting state space is
\mathcal{H}_{p,Z}=\mathcal{H}_{c}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{2}
,
and the initial state is
(p,Z)=|0\rangle\otimes|0\rangle
.
The quantum solution is calling the recursive quantum procedure

\mathit{RQFS}(p/q,Z/Y)

followed by a measurement
M^{\prime}
on the resulting qubit of
Z

(
viz.
|g(s_{(\emptyset)})\rangle
)
, with

\displaystyle M^{\prime}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\big\{M_{0}^{\prime}\triangleq|0\rangle\langle 0|,\;M_{1}^{\prime}\triangleq|1\rangle\langle 1|\big\}

In each step of recursive quantum procedure
\mathit{RQFS}(q,Y)
:

(1) Perform measurement
M
with

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\bigg\{M_{0}\triangleq\sum_{0\leq i<l}|i\rangle\langle i|,\;M_{1}\triangleq|l\rangle\langle l|,\;M_{2}\triangleq\sum_{l<i<2^{m}}|i\rangle\langle i|\bigg\}

on the counting state
q
; then execute steps (2-4) according to the measurement outcome.

(2) If the outcome is 0, perform steps (21-29).

(21):

Increment the value of
q
by 1. That is, apply
(+1)
-operator
U_{+1}
to
q
.

(22):

Introduce ancillas
\mathbb{X}[q]
,
\mathit{Y}^{\prime}
in the state
|0\rangle\otimes|0\rangle
.

(23):

Prepare
\big(\mathbb{X}[q],\mathit{Y}^{\prime}\big)
to
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{n}}}\sum_{x=0}^{2^{n}-1}|x\rangle\otimes\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(|0\rangle-|1\rangle)
by applying
H^{\otimes n}\otimes HX
.

(24):

Call
\mathit{RQFS}(q/q,Y^{\prime}/Y)
.

(25):

Apply
H^{\otimes n}
on register
\mathbb{X}[q]
.

(26):

Apply quantum oracle
\mathcal{G}
to
\mathbb{X}[q]
,
\mathit{Y}
with
\mathcal{G}
calculating
g
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\ |s\rangle|y\rangle

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle|s\rangle|y\oplus g(s)\rangle

(27):

Return
\mathbb{X}[q]
,
\mathit{Y}^{\prime}
to their original state by reversing steps (23-26).

(28):

Release ancillas
\mathbb{X}[q]
,
\mathit{Y}^{\prime}
.

(29):

Return
q
to its original state. That is, apply
(-1)
-operator
U_{-1}
to
q
.

(3) If the outcome is 1, apply quantum oracle
\mathcal{O}
to
\mathbb{X}[1],\ldots,\mathbb{X}[l]
,
\mathit{Y}
, with
\mathcal{O}
defined as

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\ |x_{1}\rangle\ldots|x_{l}\rangle|y\rangle

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle|x_{1}\rangle\ldots|x_{l}\rangle|y\oplus g(s_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{l})})\rangle

(4) If the outcome is 2, the procedure collapses (implemented by
\mathbf{bot}
).

We refine recursive quantum procedure
\mathit{RQFS}
and main program
\mathit{m}ain
by Tab.
19
.

Notations and Definitions.

Following notations and definitions will be used in the subsequent two subsections.
Let
K
be a quantum predicate variable over
\mathcal{H}_{c}
.
Let
\big\{|i\rangle\big\}_{i}
be the computational basis of
\mathcal{H}_{q}
.
For notational convenience, the
(i,i)
-component
\langle i|K|i\rangle
of
K
is abbreviated as
K_{i}

(
K=\sum_{i,j}\langle i|K|j\rangle|i\rangle\langle j|
)
.
We shall use the main-diagonal elements of
K
to encode classical information.

Define
C(i)
and
D(i)
by

\displaystyle C(i)

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\bigotimes_{j=0}^{i}\Big(\sum_{k=0}^{2^{n}-1}|k\rangle_{x_{j}}\langle k|\otimes\alpha_{j}\Big)

\displaystyle D(i)

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\bigotimes_{j=0}^{i}\Big(\sum_{k=0}^{2^{n}-1}|k\rangle_{x_{j}}\langle k|\otimes\beta_{j}\Big)

where
\alpha_{j}
and
\beta_{j}
are defined by

\displaystyle\alpha_{j}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}|0\rangle_{y_{0}}\langle 0|,&\hbox{if $j=0$}\\
|-\rangle_{y_{j}}\langle-|,&\hbox{if $1\leq j\leq l$}\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\beta_{j}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}|g(s_{(\emptyset)})\rangle_{y_{0}}\langle g(s_{(\emptyset)})|,&\hbox{if $j=0$}\\
|-\rangle_{y_{j}}\langle-|,&\hbox{if $1\leq j\leq l$}\end{array}\right.

Define
P(K)
and
Q(K)
by

\displaystyle P(K)

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{l}K_{i}|i\rangle_{q}\langle i|\otimes C(i)

\displaystyle Q(K)

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{l}K_{i}|i\rangle_{q}\langle i|\otimes D(i)

Intuitively,
P(K)

(
resp.
Q(K)
)
says that the control flow arrives
at each recursion depth
0\leq i\leq l
(denoted by variable
q
) of algorithm
\mathit{RQFS}
with probability
K_{i}

(where
i
corresponds to each level of the tree, in particular,
i=0
points to the root and
i=l
to the leaves),
and at depth
i
, variable
\mathbb{Y}[0]
lies in the state
|0\rangle

(
resp.
|g(s_{(\emptyset)})\rangle
)
,

\mathbb{Y}[j]
with
1\leq j\leq i
in
|-\rangle
,
and
\mathbb{X}[j]
with
0\leq j\leq i
can lie in any state (by the predicate
I_{x_{j}}
).

Table 20
.
Partial correctness of recursive quantum Fourier sampling.

We claim that, on any input,
the main program
\mathit{m}ain
executes with output
|0\rangle_{p}\otimes|g(s_{(\emptyset)})\rangle_{Z}
(if it terminates).
In a formal fashion, it is claimed that

Proposition F.0
.

It is the case that

(66)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{qPP}}

\displaystyle\big\{I_{p}\otimes I_{Z}\big\}\,\mathit{m}ain\,\big\{|0\rangle_{p}\langle 0|\otimes|g(s_{(\emptyset)})\rangle_{Z}\langle g(s_{(\emptyset)})|\big\}

Proof.

The proof of Ass. (
66
) is shown in Tab.
20
, where “TBA” means “To Be Announced”.
To prove Hoare’s triple (x), by (R Adap), it is sufficient to show that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{qPP}}

\displaystyle\big\{|0\rangle_{q}\langle 0|\otimes|0\rangle_{y_{0}}\langle 0|\big\}\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{RQFS}\big(q,\mathbb{Y}[q]\big)\,\big\{|0\rangle_{q}\langle 0|\otimes|g(s_{(\emptyset)})\rangle_{y_{0}}\langle g(s_{(\emptyset)})|\big\}

By definition of
P(K)
and
Q(K)
, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{qPP}}

\displaystyle\big\{P(|0\rangle_{q}\langle 0|)\big\}\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{RQFS}\big(q,\mathbb{Y}[q]\big)\,\big\{Q(|0\rangle_{q}\langle 0|)\big\}

By (R Subst), together with the substitution
[|0\rangle_{q}\langle 0|/K]
, it suffices to show that

(67)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{qPP}}

\displaystyle\{P(K)\}\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{RQFS}\big(q,\mathbb{Y}[q]\big)\,\{Q(K)\}

The proof of Ass. (
67
) is shown in Tab.
20
,
where partial correctness of the body of variable localization,
i.e. proof of Hoare’s triple (o), is shown in Tab.
20
.

To see Ass. (g), by definition of
C(i-1)
and the fact that

\displaystyle I_{x_{i}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{2^{n}-1}|k\rangle_{x_{i}}\langle k|

we remark that

\sum_{i=1}^{l}K_{i-1}|i\rangle_{q}\langle i|\otimes C(i-1)\otimes H^{\otimes n}I_{x_{i}}H^{\otimes n}\otimes|-\rangle_{y_{i}}\langle-|

is equivalent to

\begin{array}[]{c}\sum_{i=1}^{l}K_{i-1}|i\rangle_{q}\langle i|\otimes\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{i}=0}^{2^{n}-1}\bigotimes_{k=1}^{i-1}(|x_{k}\rangle\langle x_{k}|\otimes\alpha_{k})\\
\otimes\>H^{\otimes n}(-1)^{g(s_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{i})})}|x_{i}\rangle\langle x_{i}|(-1)^{g(s_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{i})})}H^{\otimes n}\otimes|-\rangle_{y_{i}}\langle-|\end{array}

By Eq.

\displaystyle g(s_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{i})})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle s_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{i-1})}\cdot x_{i}

it is to say

\begin{array}[]{c}\sum_{i=1}^{l}K_{i-1}|i\rangle_{q}\langle i|\otimes\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{i}=0}^{2^{n}-1}\bigotimes_{k=1}^{i-1}(|x_{k}\rangle\langle x_{k}|\otimes\alpha_{k})\\
\otimes\>H^{\otimes n}(-1)^{s_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{i-1})}\cdot x_{i}}|x_{i}\rangle\langle x_{i}|(-1)^{s_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{i-1})}\cdot x_{i}}H^{\otimes n}\otimes|-\rangle_{y_{i}}\langle-|\end{array}

By the fact that

\displaystyle\sum_{x}H^{\otimes n}(-1)^{x\cdot y}|x\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|y\rangle

it is equivalent to saying that

\begin{array}[]{c}\sum_{i=1}^{l}K_{i-1}|i\rangle_{q}\langle i|\otimes\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{i}=0}^{2^{n}-1}\bigotimes_{k=1}^{i-1}(|x_{k}\rangle\langle x_{k}|\otimes\alpha_{k})\\
\otimes\>|s_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{i-1})}\rangle\langle s_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{i-1})}|\otimes|-\rangle_{y_{i}}\langle-|\end{array}

To see Ass. (j), by the fact

\displaystyle H^{\otimes n}|y\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{x}(-1)^{x\cdot y}|x\rangle

we remark that

\begin{array}[]{c}\sum_{i=1}^{l}K_{i-1}|i\rangle_{q}\langle i|\otimes\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{i}=0}^{2^{n}-1}\bigotimes_{k=1}^{i-1}(|x_{k}\rangle\langle x_{k}|\otimes\alpha_{k})\\
\otimes\>H^{\otimes n}|s_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{i-1})}\rangle\langle s_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{i-1})}|H^{\otimes n}\otimes|-\rangle_{y_{i}}\langle-|\end{array}

is equivalent to

\begin{array}[]{c}\sum_{i=1}^{l}K_{i-1}|i\rangle_{q}\langle i|\otimes\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{i}=0}^{2^{n}-1}\bigotimes_{k=1}^{i-1}(|x_{k}\rangle\langle x_{k}|\otimes\alpha_{k})\\
\otimes\>(-1)^{s_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{i-1})}\cdot x_{i}}|x_{i}\rangle\langle x_{i}|(-1)^{s_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{i-1})}\cdot x_{i}}\otimes|-\rangle_{y_{i}}\langle-|\end{array}

An easy calculation yields

\begin{array}[]{c}\sum_{i=1}^{l}K_{i-1}|i\rangle_{q}\langle i|\otimes\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{i}=0}^{2^{n}-1}\bigotimes_{k=1}^{i-1}(|x_{k}\rangle\langle x_{k}|\otimes\alpha_{k})\otimes|x_{i}\rangle\langle x_{i}|\otimes|-\rangle_{y_{i}}\langle-|\end{array}

By definition of
C(i)
, it is equivalent to saying that

\sum_{i=1}^{l}K_{i-1}|i\rangle_{q}\langle i|\otimes C(i)

By definition of
P(K)
, it is exactly
P\big(\sum_{i=1}^{l}K_{i-1}|i\rangle\langle i|\big)
.

This completes the proof.
∎

F.4.
Total correctness

(a)

\langle P_{h}(K)\rangle\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{RQFS}\big(q,\mathbb{Y}[q]\big)\,\langle Q(K)\rangle

\mathit{Prem}_{h}

\langle\sum_{i=l-h}^{l-1}K_{i}|i+1\rangle_{q}\langle i+1|\otimes C(i)\otimes I_{x_{i+1}}\otimes I_{y_{i+1}}\rangle

(b)

\big(\mathbb{X}[q],\mathbb{Y}[q]\big):=|0\rangle
;

(A Init)

\langle\sum_{i=l-h}^{l-1}K_{i}|i+1\rangle_{q}\langle i+1|\otimes C(i)\otimes 0_{x_{i+1}}\otimes 0_{y_{i+1}}\rangle

(c)

\big(\mathbb{X}[q],\mathbb{Y}[q]\big)\;{\ast}{=}\;H^{\otimes n}\otimes HX;

(A Unit)

\langle\sum_{i=l-h}^{l-1}K_{i}|i+1\rangle_{q}\langle i+1|\otimes C(i)\otimes I_{x_{i+1}}\otimes|-\rangle_{y_{i+1}}\langle-|\rangle

(d)

\langle P_{h}\big(\sum_{i=l+1-h}^{l}K_{i-1}|i\rangle\langle i|\big)\rangle

{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}}

(e)

\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{RQFS}\big(q,\mathbb{Y}[q]\big)
;
\langle Q\big(\sum_{i=l+1-h}^{l}K_{i-1}|i\rangle\langle i|\big)\rangle

(a, R Subst)

(f)

{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}}

(b)

Proof of Ass. (
71
).

Table 21
.
Total correctness of recursive quantum Fourier sampling.

We claim that, on any input, the main program
main
always terminates with output

|0\rangle_{p}\otimes|g(s_{(\emptyset)})\rangle_{Z}
.
In a formal fashion, it is claimed that

Proposition F.0
.

It is the case that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{qTP}}

\displaystyle\big\langle I_{p}\otimes I_{Z}\big\rangle\,main\,\big\langle|0\rangle_{p}\langle 0|\otimes|g(s_{(\emptyset)})\rangle_{Z}\langle g(s_{(\emptyset)})|\big\rangle

Proof.

The proof is as for Prop.
F.1
, with exception of the following assertion

(68)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{qTP}}

\displaystyle\langle P(K)\rangle\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{RQFS}\big(q,\mathbb{Y}[q]\big)\,\langle Q(K)\rangle

Define a sequence of
\mathrm{PQPT}
s
\big\{P_{h}(K)\big\}_{h\geq 0}
by

\displaystyle P_{h}(K)

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{i=l+1-h}^{l}K_{i}|i\rangle_{q}\langle i|\otimes C(i)&\hbox{$0\leq h<l$}\\
P(K),&\hbox{otherwise}\end{array}\right.

It’s easy to see that

\displaystyle\models_{\mathbb{I}}

\displaystyle P_{0}(K)=0

\displaystyle\models_{\mathbb{I}}

\displaystyle P_{h}(K)\sqsubseteq P_{h+1}(K),\quad\forall h\geq 0

For the sake of space savings, define a set of premises
\big\{\mathit{Prem}_{h}\big\}_{h\geq 0}
by

\displaystyle\mathit{Prem}_{h}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\big\langle P_{h}(K)\big\rangle\,\mathbf{call}\ \mathit{RQFS}(q,\mathbb{Y}[q])\,\big\langle Q(K)\big\rangle,\quad\forall h\geq 0

To prove Ass. (
68
), by (Rt pRec), it suffices to show that

(70)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}},\mathit{Prem}_{h}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{qBE}}

\displaystyle\big\langle P_{h+1}(K)\big\rangle\,\mathbf{if}\ \Box m\cdot M[q]=m\rightarrow S_{m}\ \mathbf{fi}\,\big\langle Q(K)\big\rangle

for all
h\geq 0
.
The case of
h\geq l
has been shown in the proof of Prop.
F.1
,
while the remaining cases —
0\leq h<l
— can be uniformly dealt with as follows.

Fix
0\leq h<l
.
To prove Ass. (
70
), by (R Case), it suffices to show that

(71)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}},\mathit{Prem_{h}}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{\mathit{qBE}}}

\displaystyle\bigg\langle\sum_{i=l-h}^{l-1}K_{i}|i\rangle_{q}\langle i|\otimes C(i)\bigg\rangle\,S_{0}\,\bigg\langle Q(K)\bigg\rangle

(72)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{qBE}}

\displaystyle\big\langle K_{l}|l\rangle_{q}\langle l|\otimes C(l)\big\rangle\,S_{1}\,\big\langle Q(K)\big\rangle

(73)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathit{\sqsubseteq}}

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathit{qBE}}

\displaystyle\big\langle 0\big\rangle\,S_{2}\,\big\langle Q(K)\big\rangle

where Ass. (
73
) follows from (A Bot),
the proof of (
72
) is as in proof of Prop.
F.1
,
and, finally, the proof of (
71
) can be adapted from that of Prop.
F.1

by replacing
\sum_{i=0}^{l-1}
and
\sum_{i=1}^{l}
with
\sum_{i=l-h}^{l-1}
and
\sum_{i=l+1-h}^{l}
, respectively (cf. Tab.
21
).
∎
